Chapter 421: The Third Young Master’s Visit

This was a rather good mask; it could even be considered a treasure. Of course, this was only based on Ji Yuan’s personal standards, since he had his own criteria for judgment based on his own interests and beliefs. If it were Old Dragon or some more serious cultivators, they would think that this mask was useless, at most just a trinket.

However, as Hu Yun’s first creation, Ji Yuan gave it a positive review, handing the mask back to Hu Yun and encouraging him.

“Even if you gave him an ordinary mask, Yin Qing would like it, let alone this, which is indeed an excellent object. For it to have such an effect on your first attempt at crafting an object, your years of cultivation have been worthwhile!”

Hu Yun puffed out his chest like a human, grabbed the mask with both paws, and hesitated for a moment before speaking.

“It took me a lot of spiritual energy to make it, and I even used some of my own spiritual hairs. Actually, I failed a few times along the way, so I’m really lucky to have made it this well.”

After thinking for a moment, Hu Yun added.

“Moreover, although this mask is fun, its shortcomings are quite obvious. It requires a high level of skill from the user. One requirement is intelligence, and another is the ability to clearly understand one’s own thoughts and create a full and three-dimensional image in order to use the mask well. Otherwise, it’s just for fun.”

“Knowing humility is also a great step forward!”

Ji Yuan smiled and patted the red fox’s head. The two fox ears swished back and forth beneath his hand, then bounced up and pressed down again.

Hu Yun was so embarrassed by Ji Yuan’s praise that his already fiery red fur appeared even more vibrant and shiny. It seemed that since he could remember, the respected Mr. Ji had rarely praised him — or rather, he almost never praised him.

“I’ll go and prepare some more, I’ll give Yin Qing a surprise later!”

With that, Hu Yun ran away as if fleeing, clutching his mask. Ji Yuan nodded and smiled at his back, then turned back to continue working on the chessboard in front of him.

In the evening, Yin Zhaoxian and Yin Qing rode in the same carriage as usual, surrounded by guards and servants, returning home from the Ministry of Works.

Since today was the day of classes at the Imperial Academy, Yin Zhong returned alongside his father and elder brother.

After changing into casual clothes, the father and his two sons walked together into the courtyard of the guesthouse.

Hearing footsteps from afar, Hu Yun called out.

“Yin Qing! Yin Qing! The gift I prepared for you is finally finished, come quickly!”

Upon hearing the sound, Yin Qing smiled and immediately quickened his pace. Yin Zhong, who was beside him, was even more excited than his brother, and they ran over together.

Upon arriving at the guesthouse, Hu Yun was sitting in the courtyard flicking his tail back and forth, a clear smile on his fox face as he looked at the two brothers emerging from the corridor, coming to stand before him.

“Hehe, Yin Qing, guess what I’m giving you? Tiger can guess too!”

Yin Zhong spoke first.

“It must be something good you brought from Niu Kui Mountain, right?”

“No, you guessed wrong. Now it’s Yin Qing’s turn to guess. Each person can only guess once!”

Hu Yun was a little worried that if the two brothers kept guessing and got it right, there would be no surprise left. After hearing his words, Yin Qing thought for a moment and replied.

“It’s either delicious food or fun activities. You’ve been busy for so many days, it must be something fun, right?”

Hu Yun was very dissatisfied with his supposedly bright answer.

“That doesn’t count as a correct guess; you need to tell me exactly what it is!”

Yin Qing shrugged.

“There are countless interesting things in the world. You, Uncle Hu Yun, are a fox spirit, and your vision far surpasses that of us mortals. I, Yin Qing, am too ignorant to guess. Please, have mercy and tell us!”

“Hehe, you’re right! Well then, I’ll let you know!”

Hu Yun was quite pleased with himself. He reached one paw into his fluffy tail and pulled out a bright red fox mask, which even had a flash of light across it.

Yin Zhong took a liking to the mask almost immediately upon seeing it. He looked at his brother and then at the mask, his face full of envy.

Not to mention Yin Zhong, even Yin Qing couldn’t hide his joy when he saw the mask.

“This…is for me?”

“Here, take it. It was originally meant for you.”

Hu Yun casually handed it to Yin Qing, as if it were a trivial trinket. After Yin Qing carefully took it, Yin Zhaoxian, who had been lagging behind a few steps in the corridor, slowly strolled over.

Yin Zhaoxian glanced at the mask and saw that it had a fiery glow flowing on it, along with a faint scent that reminded him of Hu Yun.

Such tricks were naturally ineffective against Yin Zhaoxian’s righteous aura, but he wouldn’t reveal it. He understood that this must be a treasure, so he asked with curiosity.

“Wow, what a beautiful mask! Did Hu Yun make it? You’re amazing! No craftsman can match your skill!”

“Yes, what a beautiful mask! I want one too…”

Yin Zhong’s voice was filled with envy. Yin Qing, already in his thirties, was still carefully examining the mask, turning it over and around. Every detail on it was exquisite, the hair seemed to be swaying in the wind, and it was very comfortable to hold.

Hu Yun had been praised so many times today that his fur had turned even redder. He scratched his head with his paw, stood up on his hind legs, and said to Yin Qing.

“Yin Qing, this mask isn’t just an ordinary toy; there’s something special about it. Even Mr. Ji praised it. Let me teach you how to use it!”

Hu Yun explained the characteristics of the mask in detail, including how to use it, what to avoid, and the limits of its durability and realism.

“How about that? It’s pretty complicated, isn’t it? So I’ll have to guide you properly over the next few days, otherwise you won’t be able to use it, and all my hard work will be wasted!”

Hu Yun spoke earnestly, and then saw Yin Qing tentatively put on the mask.

“What you desire in your heart must three-dimensional, and you cannot omit temperament nor appearance. The same goes for your desired personality, understood?”

A few breaths after the mask had been put on, a faint fiery glow seemed to flow around Yin Qing’s body, like ripples on water. Then, his figure became blurred as if reflected in a pool, and when it became clear again, his appearance had completely changed.

Yin Zhong was startled, then his eyes widened.

“Uncle Yu?”

The elder brother who had originally standing beside him suddenly appeared to Yin Zhong as Yu Haishan, the Governor of the Capital Prefecture, though he was still wearing Yin Qing’s clothes.

“What?”

Hu Yun was also stunned. Yin Qing actually succeeded on the first try? Hu Yun himself felt that there were still many details that hadn’t been explained clearly!

Yin Zhaoxian, standing to the side, squinted and stroked his beard as he looked at Yin Qing’s current appearance. At first glance, he looked just like Yu Haishan, but he could sense the “scent of Hu Yun” on Yin Qing. Upon closer inspection, there was a faint red, monstrous glow. But he had to admit that the mask was very magical.

“Master Yin, let the children have their own fun. Come over and play a game?”

Yin Zhaoxian smiled and walked towards Ji Yuan at the table in the courtyard a little further away.

“Hahaha, that’s exactly what I was thinking!”

Approaching the stone table, he saw that Ji Yuan had already set up an unfinished game. He had actually seen this game many times before. Although there were some differences in each game, he could generally recognize that it was a similar pattern.

“Is Mr. Ji setting up this scheme again?”

“Hmm, I can’t arrange it properly. I can’t remember, never mind.”

Ji Yuan smiled and picked up the chess pieces from the board one by one and put them back into the chess box. Yin Zhaoxian also sat down and picked up the chess pieces of the other color.

…

About half an hour later, a group of guards marched in orderly steps along Rong’an Street, where the Yin residence was located. Behind them were two magnificent carriages, each surrounded by guards and servants whose attire and appearances were unusual.

The procession quickly arrived at the gate of the Yin residence and stopped. The guards at the gate knew immediately that important figures had arrived, but they were unable to distinguish any origins from the guards’ uniforms. The carriages bore no insignia, so they could not identify the visitors.

“May I ask which esteemed guest has arrived? We must go in and inform the master!”

The head guard at the gate dared not be negligent. He led his men down the steps of the Yin residence. When they reached the procession, he cupped his hands, bowed, and asked.

In the distance, on the carriage in front, the thick velvet curtain was lifted from the inside, and a man in a tall hat and black robe stepped down. The head guard at the gate felt a jolt in his heart. This was a eunuch. The person in the carriage was probably either from the palace, or related to the imperial family!

The eunuch always walked with a slight bow, seemingly taking small, quick steps, but in reality, he was quite fast. He quickly passed the guards and arrived in front of the head guard of the Yin residence. The eunuch’s slightly shrill voice rang out.

“Servant of the Prime Minister’s residence, go in and inform Lord Yin that the Third Young Master has come to visit him with his wife and daughter. Once you tell Lord Yin, he will know who it is.”

The Third Young Master?

The guard captain frowned, but dared not be negligent and bowed.

“I will go and inform the master immediately. Please wait a moment!”

“Yes, thank you for your trouble.”

After the eunuch finished speaking, he turned and walked towards the carriage. The guards at the gate did not dare to delay and quickly ran back to the mansion.

Not long after, Chen Ayuan jogged briskly to the guest wing in the backyard of the Yin residence. After glancing subconsciously at the red fox who was talking and chatting with Yin Qing and Yin Zhong, he quickly walked to the table where Yin Zhaoxian and Ji Yuan were playing chess.

Even in a hurry, Chen Ayuan slowed his pace and bowed respectfully once he reached the courtyard.

“Master, Mr. Ji, I apologize for disturbing your chess game.”

“What’s is it?”

Yin Zhaoxian put the chess piece down and turned to look at his loyal servant.

“Reporting to the master, a procession of carriages has arrived outside. It’s quite a grand affair. There’s a eunuch in the procession who informed the gate guards that the Third Young Master has come to visit with his wife and daughter.”

Yin Zhaoxian frowned.

“Third Young Master?”

“Father, it’s the Emperor!”

Yin Qing overheard the conversation and spoke up, a certainty in his guess.

“Oh right, it really must be the Emperor! Mr. Ji, it seems this game of chess between us will be a draw!”

Ji Yuan smiled and nodded at him.

“You really think we are evenly matched? I’ll win in less than twenty moves.”

“Hey, hey, how can you say that? How do you know I don’t have a backup plan? The Emperor is here, and I have to go and receive him. We’ll play another day, another day!”

Yin Zhaoxian stood up and quickly followed Chen Ayuan, bringing Yin Qing and Yin Zhong with him. Since the Emperor was coming in person, the whole family naturally had to go and greet him.

Chapter 422: Yin Qing Is Urged To Marry

“Third Young Master…”

Ji Yuan murmured to himself as he watched Yin Zhaoxian, Yin Qing, and the others hurriedly leave. He recalled first hearing that title on the Zhuangyuan Ferry across the Tongtian River outside the capital city.

“This emperor does have some sentimentality after all!”

…

At the main entrance of the Yin residence, Yin Zhaoxian, along with his wife and son and several servants, hurried to the gate. After tidying their clothes, the group went outside. The guards and carriages hadn’t moved, and the people in the carriages hadn’t disembarked, waiting for Yin Zhaoxian to arrive.

Yin Zhaoxian led his family out of the mansion gate, down the steps, and then to the carriage. Everyone bowed in greeting.

“Subject Yin Zhaoxian, along with his family and servants, pays his respects to His Majesty!”

“Greetings to His Majesty!”

The crowd behind him repeated in unison.

At this moment, the curtain on the carriage was lifted, and the guards simultaneously set out a footstool. A eunuch descended the carriage first, then helped the person following him down. It was none other than the current Emperor Hongwu,1 Yang Hao of Dazhen.

“Please rise, everyone, no need for formalities!”

Emperor Hongwu took a few steps closer to Yin Zhaoxian and personally helped him to his feet.

“Thank you, Your Majesty!”

“Hahaha, Minister Yin, I left the palace today without making a fuss. My visit to your residence is a private one, and I even brought my family with me. You may all descend as well!”

Emperor Hongwu spoke the first half to Yin Zhaoxian, and the second half to another carriage behind him. Then, the curtain of that carriage was lifted from the inside, and two women stepped out, one older but elegant and graceful, and the other beautiful and poised.

“Greetings to Consort De, and Princess Changping!”

Yin Zhaoxian and his family members behind him bowed respectfully once again.

“Everyone, there’s no need for such formalities. We hope our visit to your residence today hasn’t disturbed you?”

Consort De led the princess to the emperor’s side, smiling as she spoke to Yin Zhaoxian and his wife — particularly at the latter, since Mrs. Yin held an imperial title and occasionally visited the palace to chat.

“Not at all, Your Majesty, Your Highness, and Your Highness the Princess. It’s getting dark, and it’s freezing outside. Please come inside and warm yourselves up. I have already instructed the kitchen to prepare a sumptuous dinner.”

Regardless of the emperor’s purpose in coming, it wouldn’t be appropriate to just stand outside to discuss matters. Yin Zhaoxian invited the emperor into the mansion, having already instructed the servants and kitchen staff to make preparations.

“Very well, today I will taste the dishes at Prime Minister Yin’s residence!”

“We happen to have some special preserved meats from my hometown of Ning’an County. Your Majesty, please have a taste. This way please, Your Majesty!”

Yin Zhaoxian gestured for them to enter. Yin Qing didn’t say much throughout the process, simply following his father and keeping an eye on Yin Zhong. However, he noticed that Consort De and Princess Changping would occasionally glance at him.

This made Yin Qing frown, but after a moment’s thought, he came up with an absurd possibility.

Several braziers had already been lit in the guest hall of the Yin residence, and servants had prepared tea and pastries. As the group walked through the doors and corridors, they immediately felt a wave of warmth wash over them.

The living room was filled with calligraphy and paintings. Most of the calligraphy had been written by Yin Zhaoxian, while the paintings had mostly been done by Yin Qing. The Yin family father and son were well-known both inside and outside the court in this regard.

After everyone was seated, Emperor Hongwu’s gaze lingered on the calligraphy and paintings in the room. He admired them sincerely for a long time.

“Everyone says that Minister Yin is an honest and upright official, but the truth is that you are not short of money at all. Your house is full of treasures. Just look at the calligraphy and paintings in this living room. Countless wealthy and powerful people have tried to buy them at great expense but have failed!”2

Yin Zhaoxian sipped his tea and smiled.

“Your Majesty flatters me. Others merely flatter my work because I, Yin Zhaoxian, hold a high position!”

“Prime Minister Yin is too modest. Even I, a woman in the inner palace, have heard countless stories about you. I know that you are a great calligrapher of our time, Minister Yin, and your son, Yin Qing, is also highly renowned for his exquisite paintings. Each stroke of his brush is worth a fortune. You and your son are both exceptional talents!”

Consort De was very eloquent and well aware of Yin Zhaoxian’s standing in the Emperor’s heart. Emperor Hongwu had spoken to her many times in bed about Yin Zhaoxian, mostly praising him and even expressing admiration.3

Yin Zhaoxian was destined to become the Crown Prince’s tutor. From another perspective, if Yin Zhaoxian truly believed that any prince was incompetent and unworthy of the position, then no matter how much the Emperor favored that prince, they would not be selected as Crown Prince.

In addition, the common people and even the civil and military officials had long heard a rumor that Yin Zhaoxian was a virtuous and wise scholar of the era, with a strong sense of justice, clear understanding of the world and the ability to eliminate evil, just like what had happened with the Bloody Silk Case.

To this day, people in Lishun Prefecture, Yunbo Prefecture, and even the entire Wan Province still prayed for Yin Zhaoxian every year, and many places had erected shrines to him, giving the area a somewhat mystical feel.

“Yes, Prime Minister Yin, your talent is known throughout the court and the country, and so are Vice Minister Yin’s superb painting skills.”

Princess Changping echoed this sentiment and looked at Yin Qing, who, sitting on the edge of the room, simply bowed slightly in thanks without saying anything more.

“By the way, Minister Yin, you are already over thirty, yet you are still unmarried. Is it possible that you have an arranged marriage from childhood?”

Yin Zhaoxian glanced at Yin Qing, who was sipping his tea with his head down, and replied.

“He is not engaged.”

“Oh, so Minister Yin has someone he likes?”

When Emperor Hongwu asked Yin Qing, the latter could only answer respectfully.

“Your Majesty, this minister has no one he loves. However, I am busy with official duties and also must to find time to study, so I have no time to indulge in romance.”

Emperor Hongwu nodded.

“As expected of the son of Prime Minister Yin, you are also a pillar of the nation. But you have reached the age of marriage. The Yin family line must continue, right? What does Prime Minister Yin say?”

Yin Zhaoxian smiled and nodded in agreement.

“Your Majesty is right. Qing’er, it’s time for you to get married.”

Yin Qing had never considered marriage, partly because he was too busy and had too many things to take care of, and partly because he had certain concerns.

Firstly, the Yin family’s current status in the court and among the people was prominent but also somewhat awkward; their foundation was actually insufficient, and they could not afford to make a wrong move.

Secondly, the Yin family itself was quite unique, and they might come into contact with some supernatural matters, so choosing a wife was something that required extreme caution.

Others might not think so much, but Yin Qing considered it very deeply. He knew that he could not marry an ordinary woman now, and marrying a lady from a prestigious family would mean getting involved with her family. The previous emperor’s actions forced him to consider it over and over again.

But given the situation today, there was no way to avoid it. Yin Qing could only smile but remain silent.

Consort De was whispering to Mrs. Yin at that moment, then gave Mrs. Yin a wink, which she understood immediately.

“Oh, by the way, Qing’er, this is Princess Changping’s first time visiting the Yin residence. Why don’t you show her around the mansion?”

Yin Zhong muttered to Yin Qing.

“What’s the point of looking around on such a cold day?”

Helpless, Yin Qing could only look at Princess Changping.

“Would Your Highness like to take a stroll around?”

The latter nodded with a polite smile.

“Thank you for your trouble, Minister Yin. I am also very curious about the Yin residence.”

Since things had come to this point, Yin Qing couldn’t refuse. After Princess Changping put on her fur cloak, he took her outside.

Yin Zhong looked at his elder brother and then at the situation inside the house. He walked to his mother’s side and whispered.

“Mother, can I go with my brother?”

“What are you going to do? You’re not allowed to go!”

Hearing his mother’s words, Yin Chong frowned. His elder brother was gone, and the emperor and empress were still here. The atmosphere was oppressive and not fun.

“Then, I’ll go find Mr. Ji…”

Muttering to himself, Yin Zhong walked slowly to Yin Zhaoxian’s side and respectfully bowed to the emperor, the empress, and his father in turn.

“Your Majesty, Your Majesty, Father, Tiger feels it’s hot here and wants to go for a walk.”

“Hehehe, Tiger’s health is really good. None of my princes can compare to Tiger.”

Emperor Hongwu also liked this child who accompanied the princes in their studies. Yin Zhaoxian said, “You flatter me,” and turned to Yin Zhong.

“What’s the point of leaving? Don’t tell me you’re going to cause trouble for your brother.”

“No, that’s not it! I’m going to find Mr. Ji…”

Yin Zhong blurted out, speaking too quickly, and only lowered his voice halfway through his sentence. Yin Zhaoxian, however, did not seem to mind, shaking his head and waving his hand.

“Go ahead, go ahead.”

The Emperor didn’t know Ji Yuan, so it was fine to treat him as an ordinary friend from his hometown. Yin Zhaoxian also didn’t know that Ji Yuan knew Yan Chang, because Yan Chang had never spoken of this to outsiders. When he had presented the mooncake to the late Emperor, he had only referred to him as “an immortal”.

After Yin Chong left in a hurry, Emperor Hongwu and Yin Zhaoxian exchanged a smile, giving them a feeling of harmony between ruler and subject.

“Does Minister Yin have a guest at his home?”

“Your Majesty, there is indeed a guest here. He is a close friend from my hometown of Ning’an County. We used to be neighbors.”

A close friend?

Emperor Hongwu grew somewhat interested. The main purpose of today’s visit was for his daughter, Princess Changping. Now that she had already gone out with Yin Qing, he could talk about whatever he wanted.

“Was Minister Yin’s close friend also a scholar?”

Yin Zhaoxian thought for a moment.

“He certainly reads books, but he is not a typical scholar who studies diligently for officialdom in the conventional sense.”

“Oh, but to be friends with Minister Yin, he must be quite learned?”

As Yin Zhaoxian listened, he began to understand the meaning behind the words. He simply smiled and said to the emperor.

“Mr. Ji is certainly talented, but he had already made it clear that he had no intention of taking up any official post. Instead, he prefers to travel the world, drink tea, listen to stories, and play chess.”

Emperor Hongwu seemed to be deep in thought, and then spoke to Yin Zhaoxian.

“He seems to be a leisurely and refined gentleman. Let’s invite him to dine at the banquet later.”

Yin Zhaoxian was taken aback.

“Well, my friend is, after all, a commoner, and, moreover, he doesn’t understand the etiquette of the powerful and wealthy. I’m afraid he might offend Your Majesty, so perhaps…”

“Oh, Minister Yin, surely you don’t think I’m so petty?”

Emperor Hongwu smiled. Heaven had created favorable conditions for him, and he considered himself a wise and benevolent ruler as recorded in history, so he certainly wouldn’t mind such trivial matters.

Chapter 423: You Understand Too?

Emperor Hongwu’s magnanimity naturally pleased Yin Zhaoxian, as his subject. However, if it were anyone else, an imperial decree would have mandated an audience with the Emperor. But the person in question was Ji Yuan.

To be honest, in Yin Zhaoxian’s mind, Ji Yuan’s subjective opinion was more important than the Emperor’s.

To put it bluntly, who exactly was Mr. Ji? There was really no need for him to care all that much about giving an emperor face.

However, Yin Zhaoxian believed that based on his understanding of Ji Yuan, asking Ji Yuan to meet Emperor Hongwu as a friend would be a favor Ji Yuan would be willing to grant him. But the problem was that Yin Zhaoxian himself was somewhat reluctant to do so. So after considering and hesitating for a while, he finally approached Emperor Hongwu.

“It is a rare honor for Your Majesty to meet a commoner, and I am also impressed by Your Majesty’s magnanimity. However… Mr. Ji is rather eccentric. How about this, I will go and teach him some basic etiquette later. After all, etiquette cannot be neglected, lest we truly offend Your Majesty…”

Upon hearing this, Emperor Hongwu became even more curious. What was wrong with Prime Minister Yin? Was his friend so shameful?

If it were anyone else, Emperor Hongwu Yang Hao might have thought it was jealousy and fear that his friend would be stealing his limelight, or even that the friend was a bad person. But since it was Yin Zhaoxian before him, there was absolutely no such possibility.

Emperor Hongwu actually had a little secret that he never told anyone, not even his very close concubines like the Empress and Consort De: around the time he ascended the throne, he had two strange dreams.

The first dream was about his father, the late Emperor Yuande.

This dream was relatively simple. Emperor Yuande had just passed away, and the dream occurred before the funeral.

At that time, Yang Hao was mostly in a state of excitement and sadness. Even his sadness wasn’t entirely due to his father’s passing; a significant portion of it was due to the death of his mentor.

In his dream, Yang Hao was sleeping in bed when he was woken up. When awoke, he saw his father sitting by the bedside. Since he did not remember that his father had passed away, Yang Hao was still a little frightened by his father was staring at him in his bedroom, so he wanted to get out of bed to pay his respects.

However, Emperor Yuande’s attitude at that time was very different from his previous impression. He seemed incredibly kind, and only spoke a few words to Yang Hao before telling him that he was leaving for a faraway place. He instructed him to take good care of the imperial family of Dazhen and to consult Yin Zhaoxian if he encountered any problems. He also told Yang Hao not to forget the last words he had spoken in his bed.

As soon as Emperor Yuande left, Yang Hao woke up in bed. It was just dawn, and he naturally remembered Emperor Yuande’s dying words — his insistence on not letting Yin Zhaoxian be harmed by treacherous officials.

In his second dream, he dreamt of his childhood. After leaving the Hongwen Academy, he became a student of Li Mushu’s, and gained a mentor who cared for him deeply and possessed great foresight.

That day in Li Mushu’s study, Yang Hao, who was only about fourteen or fifteen years old, was reading. Li Mushu listened with his eyes closed, occasionally pointing out the key points in the book, while patting a stack of books on the corner of Yang Hao’s desk with one hand.

When Yang Hao came to a passage about how a ruler should behave, Li Mushu suddenly interrupted him, his eyes still closed, and leaned closer to him.

“Your Highness, I am getting old and will leave you one day, either through scheming and intrigue or by old age and illness. But Your Highness, your life is still very long. You will surely ascend the throne and hold the great power in your hands!”

“Teacher, I also think I can become emperor. That’s just my feeling.”

Yang Hao replied with a smile.

The strange thing about the dream was that Yang Hao had almost forgotten about already becoming emperor; he seemed to still be the same boy he once was. Yet even so, he had a premonition, a strong one, of his ascension to the throne.

Li Mushu nodded and moved his right hand away from the stack of books.

“Your Highness, the author of these books is a man of unparalleled talent and wisdom, unseen since the founding of the country of Dazhen. Although I may not live to see Your Highness ascend the throne, I must ensure that he is there to witness it. This man is loyal to the country and to all its people, possessing a magnificent and righteous spirit admired even by ghosts and gods!”

Li Mushu pushed a stack of books in front of the young Yang Hao; they included words such as “On the Subject of Birds” and “Understanding Justice”.

“Read these books carefully, and read them often in the future. I must go back now!”

“Yes, Teacher!”

Young Yang Hao nodded in agreement, but just as he opened the book, a sudden realization struck him: he was in his teacher’s study, so where had his teacher gone?

Looking up, he saw a dark figure standing in the dim light outside the study door. The teacher bowed to the figure before leaving.

Yang Hao then woke up. When he woke up, every detail of the dream was still vivid in his mind. He remembered it clearly. It was very likely a dream from his deceased mentor.

These two dreams made Yang Hao, who already valued Yin Zhaoxian highly, pay even more attention to the Yin family. So even though Yin Zhaoxian was well aware of the Emperor’s trust in him, Yang Hao’s trust in Yin Zhaoxian was actually deeper than Yin Zhaoxian himself realized.

…

Yin Qing led Princess Changping out of the living room and along the corridor toward the garden. Yin Qing was always eloquent, and even though he knew the Emperor’s particular intentions of having the princess in front of him, he was not too nervous.

“Your Highness, let’s take a stroll around the corridor, walk through the two gardens to see the scenery, and then wait in the dining hall. Our Yin residence is not comparable to the palace; we’ll run out of things to see after a short stroll. If we go back too early, His Majesty and my father will probably blame me.”

Princess Changping looked at Yin Qing.

“Does Minister Yin dislike walking with me?”

Yin Qing quickly explained.

“Of course not, but His Majesty and my father probably just want us to be alone for a while. It’s freezing outside, and it’s ridiculous that my mother came up with this idea. Wouldn’t it be more comfortable for us to sit and chat by the stove in the dining hall? We could even get some good food from the kitchen.”

Princess Changping was taken aback, then laughed. She couldn’t really say she liked Yin Qing, though she didn’t dislike him either, as he was still a relative stranger to her.

However, most women of marriageable age had no say in their own lives, especially those in the royal family. Even if they were favored by their fathers, they still could not make their own decisions about their destiny.

Fortunately, although Yin Qing was far inferior to Prime Minister Yin, he was still quite talented. At least, Princess Changping had seen Yin Qing’s paintings, and found his painting skills to be quite good.

“Then let’s do as Vice Minister Yin says. Let’s just stroll around and then head to the dining hall. But I’d also like to see the study in the Yin residence. It’s said to be the place with the strongest literary atmosphere in the world.”

“Very well, I will certainly grant your request. Your Highness, this way please.”

Yin Qing, with great poise, led Princess Changping on a tour of the Yin residence. They mainly strolled through the garden, then showed her the study that Princess Changping was so curious about.

The Yin residence had several studies, and Yin Qing and Yin Zhaoxian each had their own. Yin Qing could not take Princess Changping to Yin Zhaoxian’s study, but he could show her his own.

Upon entering Yin Qing’s study, Princess Changping became somewhat lost in thought.

The room was filled with portraits, some depicting rural life, others mountains and rivers, courtyards with large canopies and trees, animals like yellow dogs and foxes, scholars who wrote with a brush and bore a resemblance to Yin Qing, and even a hazy immortal riding the wind.

Each painting was incredibly lifelike, far surpassing any other works that were publicly displayed. Princess Changping could feel the unique artistic conception within each painting.

The bookshelves and desks in the study were piled high with books. Many books on several desks were open, covered in circles, dots, and annotations on every page, showing the owner’s dedication to reading.

After checking with Yin Qing, Princess Changping picked up a book at random to see what Yin Qing had written. What she saw was not merely a typical scholar’s interpretation, but also some allusions and satire. However, Yin Qing seemed to grasp the key points immediately, revealing the essence of the book’s content.

Books, paintings, unfinished paintings, unfinished writings… all of these should have left the study cluttered, but they somehow possessed a unique sense of order and charm amidst the faint scent of ink.

Princess Changping was also a wise and well-educated woman, and her heart couldn’t help but be moved.

‘He doesn’t seem like how many people claim, that he rose to power by virtue of his father’s influence…’

Princess Changping glanced at Yin Qing, who was finally showing some embarrassment.

“Minister Yin, you are so skilled at painting, when will you paint a portrait of me?”

“If the Princess commands, then Yin Qing will naturally obey. Well, we’ve been spending more time in the study than expected. Now we don’t need to go to the dining hall early. Your Highness, we really should go now, or we’ll miss our meal.”

“Yes, yes, I understand!”

Princess Changping put down the book in her hand in the same orientation as it had been before, and then stood up with a smile.

The two took another path to the dining hall, passing by the guesthouse where Ji Yuan was staying. As Princess Changping looked around, she saw a man in white sitting at a stone table in the courtyard, fiddling with a chessboard. She asked Yin Qing, whom she had become somewhat familiar with, about him.

“Minister Yin, who is that person? He doesn’t seem to be a servant in your household.”

Looking at Ji Yuan’s profile, Yin Qing explained as he led the princess away.

“This is an elder from my hometown. It’s rare for him to come to the capital to visit. He’s staying at my house for a while. He prefers peace and quiet, so we won’t go over and disturb him.”

“Hm!”

As the two passed by, Ji Yuan’s mind was no longer on the chessboard; he was just pretending to play with the pieces. The same was true for a fox crouching under the table and a paper crane that landed on Ji Yuan’s shoulder. Of course, the “Sword Intent Inscription” had not been brought out, otherwise it would have been even more lively around them.

Hu Yun peered into the distance, his face full of curiosity.

“Mr. Ji, who is the woman next to Yin Qing?”

“It must be a princess. Judging from the way he’s leading her around alone, the Emperor’s purpose in coming to the Yin residence is quite intriguing.”

Hu Yun paused for a moment, then looked up at Ji Yuan.

“This woman is going to be Yin Qing’s wife?”

These words stunned Ji Yuan, who looked down at the fox.

“You’re just a fox practicing cultivation in the mountains, how do you know so much?”

Chapter 424: Two In-Laws

Hearing Ji Yuan ask this question, Hu Yun appeared slightly smug.

“Sir, you told me and Lu Shanjun that the culture of the human race is profound and extensive. The more I see, the more I understand. It’s easy to guess that both the man and the woman are unmarried.”

“You know she’s unmarried?”

Ji Yuan asked Hu Yun with great interest. Had this the fox also developed some kind of supernatural vision, or comprehended the art of observing auras?

However, Ji Yuan was clearly mistaken. Hu Yun pointed to his own nose with his claw.

“I can smell it. Unmarried females have a different scent; they’ll emit…”

“Alright, alright…”

Ji Yuan was both amused and exasperated. This was still an animal’s method, yet it was undeniably accurate.

Hu Yun chuckled as he watched Yin Qing and the unfamiliar woman walk away. He knew, of course, that Yin Qing was no longer young and should have been considering marriage ages ago.

“Hey Mr. Ji, what do you think of this woman? Is she a good match for Yin Qing?”

“Well, that depends on Yin Qing’s own thoughts. As for this woman, she is no ordinary person; she is likely a princess of the royal family.”

Hearing Ji Yuan’s words, Hu Yun mimicked what he had recently learned from Yin Qing, stroking the fur on his chin with his paw as if he were stroking a beard, and nodded seriously as he spoke.

“A princess, huh? In terms of status, she barely passes. I just don’t know what her character is like.”

Ji Yuan shook his head, flicked his sleeve, and all the white pieces on the chessboard went into the box. With another sweep, all the black pieces joined them.

Hu Yun was still watching the man and woman at the end of the corridor. When he saw that they had turned a corner and disappeared from sight, and he immediately became a little anxious.

“No, this is such a rare opportunity, I have to go and take a look! Mr. Ji, I’ll be off now?”

Hu Yun didn’t act rashly; he first asked Ji Yuan for his opinion. If Ji Yuan disagreed, he wouldn’t dare to wander around aimlessly.

Ji Yuan waved his hand dismissively.

“Go ahead, just don’t get caught. Master Yin should be receiving the current Emperor of Dazhen right now. He is protected by his celestial purple aura, and it’s quite powerful. Although he will not cause any harm to you so long as he doesn’t intend to, it would be best if you did not get too close to the emperor.”

“Hehe, okay, I’m off then!”

Hu Yun jumped out from under the stone table, repeated his words to Ji Yuan, and prepared to leave. However, before that, the paper crane perched on Ji Yuan flapped its wings and quickly flew to the top of Hu Yun’s head. Its meaning was self-evident.

Hu Yun patted the little paper crane with his paw, and then said the same thing to Ji Yuan again.

“Then we’ll be off?”

“Go ahead, and watch carefully. Tell me when you get back!”

“Yes!”

Hu Yun responded. In a few leaps, his body disappeared. However, Ji Yuan didn’t have time for peace and quiet, as before long, Yin Zhong’s excited voice approached, along with the sound his running feet.

“Mr. Ji, Mr. Ji, something big has happened today, something really big…”

Yin Zhong ran very fast, but when he reached Ji Yuan at the end of the corridor, he wasn’t even out of breath. His face was flushed, but whether from the cold or excitement it was hard to tell.

“What big thing? Is it related to that woman your brother was with?”

“Hahaha, that’s right, Mr. Ji. I’ll tell you a secret: that woman was here on a blind date with my brother. She’s no ordinary person; she’s Princess Changping, the Emperor’s favorite!”

Yin Zhong spoke to Ji Yuan with a hint of showing off, the implication being something like: ‘Look, isn’t my family amazing? The princess is here to arrange a blind date with my brother.’

“And also…”

Yin Zhong lowered his voice as he spoke.

“The current emperor is here too. It’s a pity that none of the princes came, otherwise I would have a lot of fun. Sir, you probably haven’t seen the emperor before, have you? He usually stays in the palace, surrounded by guards.”

Ji Yuan smiled.

“I’ve met not only the current Emperor Hongwu, but also Emperor Yuande. By the way, since you have distinguished guests at your home, what brings you here?”

Yin Zhong glanced at the corridor with a grin, then lowered his voice even further.

“I just made an excuse to come find you, left the main hall, and secretly followed my brother and Princess Changping from a distance for a while. Hehehe, they stayed in the study for quite a while before coming out. I wonder what they were doing! As the saying goes, a man and a woman alone together in a room…”

Yin Zhong was still speaking eloquently and giggling when Ji Yuan reached out and flicked him on the forehead.

“Ouch!”

Yin Zhong rubbed his forehead a couple of times.

“Mr. Ji, why did you flick me?!”

Ji Yuan shook his head, and while Yin Zhong was rubbing his forehead and not paying attention, he secretly put the chessboard and chess piece box on the stone table into his sleeve, feigning anger.

“What kind of crooked ideas are you having at such a young age?”

“I don’t…”

Yin Zhong muttered something guiltily, then suddenly saw his father, Yin Zhaoxian, coming from the other end of the corridor. He was so frightened that he shrank back and instinctively hid behind the stone table.

Yin Zhaoxian’s gait appeared unhurried, but in fact his strides were quite large, so he moved at a fast pace. When he arrived at the guesthouse courtyard, the first thing he looked at was the stone table.

“Tiger, what are you doing?”

“Uh, I… I’m looking for Hu Yun. Strange, where did that fox go?”

Yin Zhong scratched his head, half-jokingly, and stood up. Yin Zhaoxian shook his head and stopped looking at him, turning to Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, you must already know who has come today. The Emperor knows you are a guest at my home and wishes to see you. A banquet will be held in the dining hall later, and he wanted me to bring you there. I declined, saying that you are a commoner from the countryside and do not know etiquette, so I planned to…”

Ji Yuan raised his hand to stop Yin Zhaoxian from continuing, and smiled.

“Since Emperor Hongwu wishes to see me, then I will go and see him. After all, he is the Emperor of the Dynasty of Dazhen, and you, as a minister, should give him face.”

“Is…is this appropriate?”

Yin Zhaoxian was surprised that Ji Yuan was actually willing to meet the emperor, but after hearing this, he felt that it might just be for the sake of the Yin family.

“There’s nothing inappropriate about it. It’s just a meal, and it doesn’t matter who you eat with. Master Yin, you don’t need to think too much about it. Just as you said, treat me as a commoner who has never seen the emperor before and wants to catch a glimpse of him.”

Seeing that Ji Yuan really didn’t mind, that was naturally the best outcome, and Yin Zhaoxian was relieved.

“Very well, then please come with me to the dining hall, sir. The kitchen has finished its preparations. Oh, and by the way, in front of the Emperor, uh, I hope you can address him as Your Majesty…”

Ji Yuan would never be lacking in courtesy even when dealing with ordinary people, let alone the emperor, so he smiled and comforted him.

“Don’t worry, I know what I’m doing. By the way, did the Emperor come here today specifically for Yin Qing?”

Yin Zhaoxian glanced at Yin Zhong and nodded to Ji Yuan.

“He even brought along Princess Changping… If this matter succeeds, it would be quite a remarkable story!”

The two walked and talked in hushed tones, accompanied by Yin Zhong, who seemed to be following obediently but was actually listening very attentively. The three of them headed towards the dining hall.

Before long, we arrived at the door to the dining hall, along the corridor. The most conspicuous feature was the several armed guards standing meticulously in front of the door and in the courtyard.

Upon seeing Yin Zhaoxian approach, the guards bowed to him. Then the group focused their attention on Ji Yuan, observing his gait and clothing.

So long as the guards didn’t search him, Ji Yuan would ignore all their scrutinizing gazes, and Yin Zhaoxian did the same, reaching out to invite him in near the doorway.

“Please come in, Mr. Ji. His Majesty and Consort De are already inside.”

The guards opened the door, and Yin Zhaoxian and Ji Yuan entered one after the other, followed by Yin Zhong.

This dining hall was very large and could accommodate several tables of food and drink. At this moment, only one table had been set up, making the room appear quite spacious. From the doorway, there were about four or five zhang1 of room before the dining table. Emperor Hongwu sat at the head of the table, watching the entrance, with maids, eunuchs, and several guards in between.

The other party was now the emperor, which was a very special existence, and Ji Yuan could see this clearly; Yang Hao was much more dignified than before.

Yin Zhong ran straight to his mother’s side without any hesitation. Everyone, including the Emperor, just watched with smiles, while Yin Zhaoxian stood at the entrance and bowed to the emperor.

“Your Majesty, this is my friend…”

As he spoke, Yin Zhaoxian glanced at Ji Yuan and whispered a reminder to him.

“Mr. Ji…”

Ji Yuan didn’t need any further explanation. He smiled and gave Emperor Hongwu a slight bow.

“This humble subject, Ji Yuan, greets His Majesty.”

Emperor Hongwu was also carefully observing Ji Yuan. He saw that although the newcomer was dressed simply, he had a leisurely and elegant demeanor. His bearing was not inferior standing next to the distinguished Yin Zhaoxian. This first impression made him certain that he was indeed a remarkable person of great importance.

“Come, come, Minister Yin and Mr. Ji, there’s no need for formalities. Please take your seats. I’m starving and eager to try the dishes from Ning’an County!”

Yin Qing, who had already taken his seat a few minutes earlier, was slightly surprised. He hadn’t expected Mr. Ji to actually come. But looking at the way Mr. Ji had glanced at him and Princess Changping a few more times, he wondered to herself:

‘Mr. Ji, you’re not here to stir up trouble because of me and Princess Changping, are you?’

As soon as Ji Yuan and Yin Zhaoxian arrived, everyone was finally present, and the kitchen immediately began serving dishes. The small doors of the side rooms were opened, and servants from the Yin family brought up steaming, fragrant dishes.

At banquets, it was inevitable to toast the Emperor, but Ji Yuan wasn’t interested and simply drank his wine. Surprisingly, Emperor Hongwu wasn’t angry about this at all; instead, he just thought about how Yin Zhaoxian was right.

Dining with the emperor wasn’t as extravagant as ordinary people imagined. Everyone has two eyes and one mouth, and they chatted about interesting and strange things while eating just like ordinary people. Since the main characters today were Yin Qing and Princess Changping, most of the conversation at the table revolved around them.

The Emperor and Consort De would tell stories about Princess Changping’s childhood, praising her intelligence, grace, and proficiency in the four arts of music, chess, calligraphy, and painting. The Yin family would also talk about how sensible and intelligent Yin Qing was as a child, with a photographic memory. Although he could be mischievous at times, no one disliked him, and even small animals were fond of him.

‘This is basically just two families who like each other and are ready to become in-laws, chatting happily at the dinner table. Judging from this situation, this marriage was arranged entirely by the parents and matchmakers; Yin Qing and Princess Changping had nothing to do with it!’

Ji Yuan shook his head, drank the wine in his cup, and thought about it with a mixture of amusement and helplessness.

Chapter 425: Quite the Charmer!

Although Ji Yuan could only be considered a companion, and sat at the edge of the table rather than in the central seats, and although Emperor Hongwu barely spoke to Ji Yuan after those few words at the beginning and was busy chatting with his prospective in-laws as both sides worked together to steer Yin Qing and Princess Changping towards their own emotions…

That did not mean that those present did not pay attention to Ji Yuan. In fact, Emperor Hongwu paid quite a bit of attention to this commoner, and the Yin family even more so.

However, the Emperor was observing secretly. The Yin family, meanwhile, was afraid that Ji Yuan would dislike such social occasions, so they did not engage him in conversation so as to avoid disturbing him.

Seeing Ji Yuan drinking alone and shaking his head with a half-smile, Emperor Hongwu, who had just remarked about how Princess Changping’s learning surpassed that of several of the princes, suddenly steered the conversation towards Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, why are you shaking your head? I heard from Yin Qing that he considers you his elder, which shows that you have a very close relationship with the Yin family. Don’t you think my Princess Changping and Yin Qing look like a perfect match?”

When the Emperor first arrived at the Yin residence, he tried to be somewhat discreet, concealing his intentions by having Yin Qing take the princess for a walk, without explicitly stating his purpose. Now, however, the conversation at the dinner table was lively, and Yin Qing and Princess Changming had not had any excessive reactions. It seemed that the Emperor and the Yin family, who expressed interest after learning the reason for the emperor’s visit, had all finally laid their cards on the table.

Upon hearing the question, Ji Yuan turned to face the Emperor and bowed slightly.

“Both Yin Qing and Her Highness the Princess are intelligent and talented. If they could be together, it would be a match made in heaven. I have no objections.”

No matter what, this was a matter between the two families. Seeing that Yin Qing and Princess Changping, the two parties involved, were eating quietly and occasionally smiling and agreeing, it was none of Ji Yuan’s business to meddle in this matter.

“Oh, so then what Mr. Ji was thinking about just now had nothing to do with Yin Qing and Changping. What was of such interest to you, could you tell me about it?”

The Emperor pressed for an answer, and Ji Yuan replied with a smile.

“Nothing, really. Just now, when I heard you talking about how the children were when they were young, I was reminded of Yin Qing from all that time ago. But I was lost in thought and only just came back to my senses.”

Emperor Hongwu nodded.

“It seems that Mr. Ji and the Yin family indeed have a deep connection and friendship.”

“Haha, when I first settled in Ning’an County, Master Yin was my only friend in the county, so naturally he helped resolve many of my troubles.”

Princess Changping, being a perceptive person, managed to steer the conversation in a different direction. She was also curious about Ji Yuan and the Yin family’s past. Upon hearing a certain word fall from Ji Yuan’s lips, she asked about it.

“Mr. Ji addresses Prime Minister Yin as ‘Master Yin’?”

It should be noted that nowadays, both inside and outside the court, everyone respectfully addressed Yin Zhaoxian as “Lord Yin” or “Prime Minister Yin,” even most the imperial relatives. In some places, he was even called “Star Scholar Yin.” Ji Yuan addressing him as “Master Yin” was quite unusual.

This was originally just a matter of habit for Ji Yuan, but after thinking about it carefully for a moment, he felt that it was not so simple a reason. After considering it for a moment, he spoke.

“Back then, Master Yin was the teacher of the county school, and everyone in Ning’an County respectfully called him Master Yin. Now, although he is the prime minister of the country, I still consider him both an eminent prime minister who skillfully governs the realm, and a great scholar who cares about education. Therefore, I respectfully address him as ‘Master Yin’.”

“I see!”

Looking at Ji Yuan’s composed demeanor, even without a detailed examination Emperor Hongwu increasingly felt that Ji Yuan was a talented individual. As the saying went, “great hermits live in the city”;1 to be close friends with Yin Zhaoxian, his talent must be quite remarkable.

Although Yin Zhaoxian had previously stated that Ji Yuan disliked the thought of being an official, Emperor Hongwu still couldn’t resist extending an olive branch.

“Mr. Ji, have you ever participated in the imperial examinations? What rank did you achieve?”

No one present was stupid. Upon hearing the question, even Yin Zhong felt that the Emperor was showing interest in prospective talent.

Ji Yuan found it amusing; he had indeed taken the college entrance examination2 in his previous life, but he shook his head and replied.

“I have never participated in the imperial examinations,3 nor do I intend to. I know I am not cut out to be an official.”

“Oh…”

Emperor Hongwu nodded, not angry, but did not give up.

“Sir, you and Minister Yin are good friends, and I imagine you are also an accomplished scholar. I wonder if you have written any books or poems?”

What kind of books had Ji Yuan written? Most of them were about supernatural powers and spiritual techniques.

“In response to Your Majesty, I have not written any books or biographies, nor do I possess any particularly outstanding talents. My friendship with Master Yin is merely an old friendship.”

Ji Yuan gave a rather formulaic answer.

“Alas, it seems that you truly have no intention of entering officialdom!”

Emperor Hongwu sighed softly. He did not believe that Ji Yuan lacked talent; even just his calm demeanor in front of the Emperor was a rare quality few could possess. This man’s excuses might truly indicate that he had no interest in serving the court or the country.

Emperor Hongwu did not want to press too much, especially with Yin Zhaoxian present, so he had no choice but to let it go.

“Come, everyone, don’t just stand there, start eating! I was just asking casually at the dinner table. The most important thing today is still my Princess Changping and Yin Qing.”

“Yes, yes, yes, let’s eat!”

Yin Zhaoxian breathed a sigh of relief. He hadn’t felt this tense adrenaline-filled feeling in a long time. He had been a little afraid that the emperor grow angry, not because he was worried that he would punish Ji Yuan, but rather because he was afraid that Ji Yuan would be angered as well.

Princess Changping and Yin Qing sat next to each other at the dinner table. In this feudal society, it was very impolite to arrange the seats like this before the man and woman were married, so this could only be described as a deliberate arrangement.

Seeing her father and Prime Minister Yin chatting once again at the dinner table, and her mother and Lady Yin also chatting with smiles on their faces, Princess Changming quietly approached Yin Qing without turning her face, but her voice carried softly to him.

“Minister Yin, is it true that this Mr. Ji has never participated in the imperial examinations nor written any books? According to his previous introduction, I heard that he only lives in a small courtyard in Ning’an County. So what exactly does he do, and where does his income come from?”

Such questions were a bit too probing and impolite, so Princess Changping didn’t ask them in public. Instead, she asked Yin Qing, whom she knew better, in private.

Yin Qing was slightly taken aback and turned to look at Princess Changping. This princess was indeed intelligent. Livelihood was a major concern for ordinary people, but it was a small matter for a pampered princess who had never worried about food and clothing since childhood. It was something that should have been quite unlikely for her to have considered.

At this moment, a servant from the Yin family brought up two plates of steaming hot crispy chicken. As the seasonings were plentiful and the chicken had just come out of the pot, the aroma of seasoned chicken filled the room, overpowering the other dishes on the table.

Just as Yin Qing was about to find an excuse to answer Princess Changping, a loud crash sounded outside.

Almost simultaneously, another voice rang out: “Ouch…”

The sound came suddenly, and the people at the table fell silent, while the guards around them tensed up slightly, remaining fully focused. Some of them shifted their feet slightly, ready to rush to the window at any moment. They believed that their fellow guards outside had already taken action, with someone specifically sent to investigate the location from which the sound came.

Yin Qing’s heart tightened. He recognized Hu Yun’s voice and subconsciously looked at Ji Yuan, only to find the latter sighing slightly while smiling. Noticing Yin Qing glancing over, Ji Yuan nodded towards the crispy chicken.

Outside, Hu Yun, as a fox, was too short and didn’t want to scratch the walls of the Yin family’s residence with his claws. So as he secretly observed from the shadows, he used a thick wooden stick to prop up the window and squatted on it. He used his limited supernatural abilities to observe the situation inside through the window paper.

With Hu Yun’s abilities, maintaining balance wouldn’t have been a problem, but once the crispy chicken arrived, even the fox spirit was completely captivated by the scent.

Having fallen, Hu Yun hurriedly fled, but his mind was filled with images and aromas of crispy chicken, and his heart filled with indignation.

‘It’s not fair, it’s not fair! Everyone else gets to eat, but me and the little paper crane don’t get anything… wait, the little paper crane doesn’t need to eat, it’s just me who doesn’t get anything!’

The fox leaped onto the roof of the dining hall, but as soon as it reached the top, it saw two guards with swords leaping up from the ridge as lightly as swallows, their movements as swift as ghosts, without making a sound.

‘Oh no!’

Hu Yun quickly flicked his tail and jumped off the roof in another direction. In his panic, he used his monstrous power, and the two guards only saw a red shadow leap off the roof.

A moment later, a guard entered the dining hall to report.

“Your Majesty, the sound just now came from a fiery red cat, and there was nothing else unusual about it.”

“Hahaha… So it’s just a cat. Look how nervous these guards are. Could there be an assassin in Prime Minister Yin’s house?”

Emperor Hongwu smiled and tried to ease the tension. Yin Zhaoxian quickly spoke up.

“Alas, Your Majesty, you are mistaken; these guards are utterly loyal, and one can never be too careful when protecting the Emperor!”

Yin Qing couldn’t help but chuckle at the thought of Hu Yun craving crispy chicken. Princess Changping looked at him and leaned closer to ask.

“Does Vice Minister Yin know that cat?”

“Yes, I am quite familiar with him! He’s a glutton, especially fond of chicken. I guess he was tempted by the aroma of the crispy chicken that was just served.”

“You have a red cat in your household? I’ve never seen a cat like that before.”

“The world is vast and full of wonders!”

Yin Qing casually made up a story with a smile. Then he dipped his finger in the wine and traced the outline of a cat on the table.

“This is a beautiful drawing! I’m even more excited for Minister Yin to paint my portrait!”

“I will make sure Your Highness is satisfied!”

Neither of them noticed that the Emperor, Consort De, and several members of the Yin family were also quietly focusing their attention on them. They were overjoyed to see the two chatting and laughing in private.

Even Ji Yuan was slightly surprised. He opened his supernatural eyes wider to observe them, and noticed that the two’s auras had become quite compatible in a short period of time.

‘This kid is quite the charmer!’

The full proverb is “The mediocre hermit resides in the forest; the great hermit lives in the city.” In short, it means that true wisdom comes not from removing oneself from all distractions (i.e. going to the woods) but from remaining disciplined despite them (i.e. staying in the city)

Play on words, the word JY uses is 高考 (gāokǎo) which in modern times now means “college entrance exam” but used to refer to the entrance exam for senior government posts, LOL

Now the word used is 科举 (kējǔ) for “imperial examination,” which is also what the Emperor used

Chapter 426: So Intelligent

Seeing the current situation, Ji Yuan felt that the matter between Yin Qing and Princess Changping was basically settled. Fortunately, the two of them did not seem to have any resistance, and Princess Changping even seemed to admire Yin Qing a little.

However, Yin Qing was already over thirty years old, while Princess Changping, although definitely one of the oldest among the princes and princesses, was only eighteen. Yin Qing was definitely an old cow eating young grass.1

After the meal, everyone was satisfied, and it was the perfect time to press their advantage. Emperor Hongwu, using his good mood as an excuse, wanted to stay at the Yin residence for a longer chat. The people of the Yin residence naturally cooperated. How did they cooperate? Of course, they sent Yin Qing and Princess Changping away so they could have more time alone.

Yin Qing and Princess Changping were not particularly resistant. Since things were like this, the latter suggested that Yin Qing could paint her portrait directly. After obtaining Yin Qing’s consent, the two of them, along with two maids, went to Yin Qing’s study.

The reception hall was warm and comfortable thanks to the stove. A small table was placed on a plush couch, and a chessboard was placed on it. Emperor Hongwu and Yin Zhaoxian sat on either side playing chess.

Consort De and Mother Yin were chatting on a couch on the other side, smiling as they talked about Yin Qing and Princess Changping. The two had already begun discussing wedding preparations and what color fabric to use for their grandchildren’s clothes. Clearly, their thoughts were progressing much faster than their actual plans.

Ji Yuan did not leave immediately after finishing his meal, but politely sat with Yin Zhong for a while before bidding farewell and leaving the guest hall alongside the equally bored Yin Zhong.

Once outside, Ji Yuan led Yin Zhong into the corridor, heading towards the garden and guest wing. The guards outside only glanced at Ji Yuan before leaving him alone, clearly determining him to be a weak and powerless scholar.

Once Yin Zhong was outside, he had no more scruples and, before he had walked too far, he hurriedly asked Ji Yuan a question.

“Mr. Ji, do you think my brother will really be getting married? I’m going to be an uncle! Princess Changping is quite beautiful. I don’t believe my brother can resist her. After all, food and sex are basic human needs!”

Ji Yuan almost flicked Yin Zhong’s forehead again.

“You little rascal, you’ve picked up quite a bit of nonsense. Qing’er was much more well-behaved than you when he was little.”

“I am not as good as my brother anyway. Besides, Mr. Ji, you might not be aware, but despite his scholarly appearance, my brother is quite strong. By the way, you still haven’t told me whether my brother can succeed at getting married this time.”

Yin Zhong was extremely interested in whether Yin Qing could get married; or rather, he was extremely interested in his future nephew. He seemed to think that once his nephew was older, they could play together at the mansion. However, he didn’t realize that by the time his nephew was old enough to run and play, Yin Zhong would be past the age where he could be mischievous and cause trouble at will.

As Ji Yuan walked along the corridor, he gazed at the stars and moon in the sky outside, and answered with a smile.

“It won’t be as fast as you think, but at this point in time the marriage between Yin Qing and that princess is probably a sure thing. After all, the Emperor and your family both want to make it happen, and Yin Qing and Princess Changping don’t seem to have any objections.”

Yin Zhong was delighted to hear this. Since Mr. Ji had said so, the matter was basically settled.

“Mr. Ji, could we please see what my brother and the Princess are doing?”

Ji Yuan looked at him.

“What else could they be doing? Painting, of course. Didn’t you hear them leaving just now?”

“I know, I know, I just want to see how they are painting. Mr. Ji, Could you use some kind of magic so we can see them without my brother finding out?”

“You little rascal, you’ve got quite a few crooked ideas. I hope you can use them for military strategy in the future!”

Ji Yuan seemingly gave an irrelevant answer before replying.

“Your brother is shrewd and a remarkable person. There are few things you can fool or hide from him. Don’t try anything funny in front of him!”

Yin Zhong had a pained expression on his face.

“I’ve experienced that countless times since I was younger. But with you here, how could he possibly see through you?”

“Hah, I’m not interested!”

Ji Yuan laughed and replied, then stopped talking and quickened his pace toward the guesthouse. Yin Zhong had no choice but to follow.

…

In Yin Qing’s study, he had already prepared the Four Treasures of the Study, including several inkstones and inks of different colors, including vermilion and orpiment.2

Princess Changping watched Yin Qing’s preparations with a curious expression, and then saw him spread a blank roll of Xuan paper on the desk.

“Minister Yin, what should I do? Should I just stand still and wait for you to finish drawing?”

As Yin Qing, who was using paperweights to press down the edges of the paper, looked up at Princess Changping and met her bright eyes.

“No need, standing all the time is tiring. You and your maid can rest on the soft couch to the side, or play chess or card games3 together.”

“Would that be alright? I’ve heard that one shouldn’t move around too much when painting portraits.”

Yin Qing tucked his sleeves in, picked up a fine brush, dipped it in ink, and tested the color on some papers nearby, answering with great confidence.

“My painting style is slightly different from that of ordinary people. Your Highness, please be as you like. What I want to paint is not just a lifeless portrait, but to capture Your Highness’s every smile and frown. So the more natural Your Highness appears, the better!”

Princess Changping’s eyes widened slightly, then she smiled and said to Yin Qing.

“Minister Yin, you can just call me Yang Ping. Hmm, I’ll call you Yin Qing from now on.”

With those words, Princess Changping called to her maid and they all sat down on the soft couch opposite the desk. They took out the chess and card games from the study, and began to play, passing the time as they nibbled on the snacks and tea that had been prepared beforehand. However, Yang Ping and the maid would occasionally glance at Yin Qing to see if he had started painting.

Yin Qing simply stood in front of the desk, gazing at the person opposite for a while. Only when he was calm and composed did he begin to paint. His eyes seemed to capture the essence of an individual, leaving an impression on them. Every stroke flowed incredibly smoothly.

While the maids were playing card games on the soft couch over to the side, they secretly observed Yin Qing, and couldn’t help but say to Yang Ping.

“Your Highness, Minister Yin is painting so diligently!”

“Hmm, play your card quickly!”

Princess Changping urged her maid to play her cards, while glancing at Yin Qing out of the corner of her eye. He would look at her for a while and then sit down and draw for a long time, instead of simply glancing at her and drawing a single stroke.

‘I wonder how the painting is going…’

Just as she was thinking this, she happened to meet Yin Qing’s gaze as he looked up. Yang Ping panicked and quickly looked away, continuing to play with the card game.

On the roof beam inside the room, a paper crane continued watching what was happening below. Sometimes it would look at Yin Qing, sometimes at Princess Changping, sometimes it would focus on Yin Qing’s painting, and sometimes it would watch Princess Changping and her maids playing a card game very carefully.

Behind the little paper crane was a ball of fiery red fluff: which was a red fox curled up in a ball.

Hu Yun also crouched on the roof beam, looking down and occasionally sniffing the air.

“Little paper crane, you found a perfect spot!”

Hu Yun’s voice was barely audible. Upon hearing this, the little paper crane tilted its head to look at Hu Yun, then turned back to focus intently on the scene below.

Before long, Hu Yun’s fox-like face stretched into a wide grin, even revealing sharp teeth, but he didn’t look scary at all. Instead, for a moment, he gave off a lewd feeling.

“Hehehe, the princess is excited. This is their first time meeting. As expected of Yin Qing, he’s so quick!”

The little paper crane tilted its head to look at Hu Yun again, and this time it didn’t turn back for a long time, leaving Hu Yun somewhat bewildered. After a while, he suddenly realized what was going on, and then answered in a very low voice but with a clear hint of an amused smile.

“Of course I could tell by smell! You can’t tell male from female paper cranes, you wouldn’t understand!”

Upon hearing this, the little paper crane turned its head again, this time focusing intently on Princess Changping Yang Ping.

About an hour and a half later, Emperor Hongwu was finally ready to return to the palace. However, when he sent someone to inform Princess Changping, Yin Qing’s painting was far from finished. This made Yang Ping a little anxious, as she did not want the painting to be affected.

However, Yin Qing was good at comforting people, and said to her with a confident smile.

“Princess, please return to the palace with peace of mind. Every smile and frown of yours is etched in my heart, and I will ensure that the painting is flawless!”

This smile successfully dispelled Yang Ping’s anxiety and amplified the feelings in her heart that she herself was not yet aware of.

“Then I’ll wait for your good news in the palace. By the way, call me Yang Ping!”

After saying this, Princess Changping left with her maids, and Yin Qing naturally accompanied her to see her off.

…

Emperor Hongwu’s carriage had already left the Yin residence, and outside, guards were jogging alongside in the cold wind. Inside the two carriages were charcoal stoves, hot tea, and snacks.

In the second carriage, Princess Changping and Consort De sat together, the mother and daughter whispering to each other.

“Ping’er, tell your mother honestly, what do you think of Minister Yin?”

Princess Changping dared not look at Consort De, fiddling with the tea and snacks, her mind preoccupied with the painting. She wondered how Yin Qing’s work was progressing, and the phrase “every frown and smile of yours is etched in my heart” kept coming back to her. A smile involuntarily appeared on her lips.

No one knew a daughter better than her mother, and Consort De was overjoyed to see this.

“So, our Ping’er has taken a liking to him?”

“Mother, what are you saying? I haven’t…”

…

At the same time, in the guest courtyard of the Yin residence, Yin Zhong had already been sent back to sleep by Ji Yuan. Now, only Ji Yuan, the Paper Crane, Hu Yun, and several of the small calligraphy spirits who had been instructed to “go out for a breath of fresh air” were in the room.

Unlike usual, the room was completely quiet; everyone was listening to Hu Yun talk.

When Hu Yun finished speaking, even Ji Yuan looked puzzled.

“Qing’er is such a capable kid!”

“Yeah, I never expected that!”

Hu Yun waved his paws excitedly, then said very seriously.

“I need to learn this skill too; I’ll need it sooner or later!”

Ji Yuan smiled, unexpectedly agreeing with this.

“That’s right. If he writes a book on this, it might spread even faster than Master Yin’s classic works!”

“Haha, Mr. Ji, do you want to learn too?”

“What utter nonsense are you spouting?”

With a flick of his sleeve, Ji Yuan swept Hu Yun aside like a ball, sending him rolling to the door of the guesthouse. The door opened at the same instant, and he rolled out of the room.

The group of small calligraphy spirits immediately flew to the door, laughing and joking at Hu Yun.

“Damn it, Hu Yun is a bastard!”

“Hahaha, serves you right!”

“You scoundrel, how dare you slander our Master!”

“You damned fox, Little Hu Yun!”

“Rolling like a ball!”

“Hahahaha…”

Just as Hu Yun was about to retort, the door slammed shut, and Ji Yuan’s voice drifted back out.

“Go back to your room and go to sleep.”
